Output peaks around 12:00 local in every timezone, so I convert to UTC before applying the 2024-03-15 window. I accept two formats (`2024-03-15 …` and `15.03.2024 …`); impossible ones like `2024-13-40 88:99` and blank or unparseable values are dropped.\n- **Units:** power readings (kW) are multiplied by the farm's usual gap between readings (5, 15 or 30 minutes). Energy readings are converted to kWh from Wh, MWh or BTU.\n- **Calibration and negatives:** I subtract the offset first, then drop anything negative. Taken literally, this means a `-999` error reading is *kept* for farms with a large negative offset; for FARM_E it becomes about 14 kWh per reading.\n- **Duplicates:** `drop_duplicates()` alone removes nothing, because no rows are exact copies. Each farm has about 3 timestamps that appear twice, with one copy holding a glitch value of `888888.8` and the other a realistic reading. I drop the glitch copy and keep the real one.\n- **Safety rule:** no inverter temperature goes above 85 °C (the maximum is about 74 °C), so the 45-minute shutdown rule never removes anything.\n- **Maintenance:** I exclude the two completed windows and keep the cancelled Sakura one.\n  - Vistula (FARM_F): 12:00–14:00 local on the 15th.\n  - Sakura (FARM_C): the firmware update on the 16th from 08:00 local. Tokyo is 9 hours ahead, so its first hour, 08:00–09:00 local (23:00–24:00 UTC), falls inside the March 15 UTC day.\n\n**Decision for you:** I treated maintenance windows as including the start time and excluding the end time, the same way the reporting day is defined. That only matters for FARM_F's 14:00 reading. If \"finishing at 14:00\" should include that reading, FARM_F drops by about 154 kWh, from 3308 to roughly 3154.","ttft_ms":3049,"type":"result","duration_ms":966493,"uuid":"a7f1c01e-0246-4e1a-9f87-ee14c465f193","ttft_stream_ms":3048,"time_to_request_ms":77,"first_content_frame_ms":3048,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
